# Language breadth and depth — the plan (vision 37, ADR-048)

Grammar v2 (`docs/plans/README.md`). A row flips to `[x]` only when
`fathom verify --service fathom --plan docs/plans/language-breadth.md` proves it.
Design: `docs/design/language-breadth.md` (section numbers below are its; §13 is the worker
recipe). Queue: `docs/plans/language-breadth.queue.json` — one `LB-Q-<id>` row per entry,
in the same order (`TestLanguageQueueMatchesPlan`, LB-F22, holds the two together).
Invariants I54 and I55 rise from `stated` as LB-F12, LB-F14, LB-F16 and LB-F17 land.

Rows name the contract they will be proven by: the function that will carry the behaviour
and the test that will reach it, or — for a language or tool format — a lexical oracle on
the committed reading whose `level_met` the harness computes (design §10). Nobody types a
level into a reading. Until the code and readings exist every row is open, and
`fathom verify` reports it open, never proven.

Row ids are stable. Foundation rows are `LB-F<n>` (Opus), measurements `LB-M<n>` (op), and
queue rows `LB-Q-<queue id>` (Sonnet workers, one per row, recipe §13). A queue row that
cannot reach its target keeps its contract and gains a `[-]` sibling
`LB-Q-<id>-rest` with a disposition (§14.2); the pattern is never loosened.

## F. Foundation — measurement first, then the engines (Opus)

### F.9 Route references (EXCELLENCE T5, wave px)

The runtime topology's first edges: a route, upstream or mesh rule to the Kubernetes Service or
compose service its target host names (design §4, "Route references"). Interim divergence from
ADR-050 (Revisit when: SG-2 lands): stored as file-tier typed edges under `route-ref`, each
carrying the `deploy:` key SG-21/SG-10 will write into `system_links`.

### F.12 Policy references (EXCELLENCE T4, wave pc)

A policy to what it governs (design §4, "Policy references"). F61 names `policy_ref` and forbids a
new edge table before RT-M1 and SG-2, so the edges are stored as route-ref's are: file-tier typed
edges under `policy-ref` (Revisit when: RT-M1 reads PASS and SG-2 lands). The kyverno and
gatekeeper items emit `resource-kind` and `label-selector` targets; the native and cloud items emit
the same contract (`resource-type`, `aws-arn`, `service-name` too) into the one resolver.

- [x] Kyverno and Gatekeeper files are claimed by API group before the Kubernetes verdict: a `.yaml`/`.yml` whose every object document is `kyverno.io`, `policies.kyverno.io` or a `cli.kyverno.io` Test reads through the kyverno dialect, `templates.gatekeeper.sh`, `constraints.gatekeeper.sh` or a `test.gatekeeper.sh` Suite through the gatekeeper dialect; a file mixing them with other objects stays Kubernetes; one hook, `policyDispatch`, in `resolveExtractor` and in `ReingestFile` (after gatewayDispatch), the dispatched extractor adding each match block's policy_ref targets {id: LB-F134, symbol: policyDispatch, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestIngest_PolicyRefs, needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9"}

### F.16 IaC: Kubernetes ecosystem and Ansible (EXCELLENCE T3, wave iac)

Argo CD and Workflows, Flux, Crossplane and Ansible, four doc-engine dialects with cross-file edges at L3
(tier C, good: no offline tool prints an entity and a line). The three CR dialects are claimed by their
content sniff when EVERY document of a file is theirs (`crDispatch`); a mixed file stays Kubernetes whole
and is counted. Ansible is the classifier's last signal. Observations O405 to O409.

### F.18 Go call grading (wave le, item go)

Go's L2 was graded on goextract's syntactic Calls, a set no tool reads. It now grades the calls the product serves
(`callgraph.BuildBoth`: the syntactic Calls that reach a node plus the typed pass's edges) against the scip-go
call pairs whose callee the subject defines; the decision, every option's numbers and the revisit triggers are
design §1.6. O39/O40 are closed in fact: no selector-method or dispatch shape is a gap (DG-21), and what the
index sees and the served graph does not is a call through a func-typed field, variable or parameter and an
interface method no in-service type implements (O446). The Go row's L2 stayed unmet on precision (.7794) in wave le; wave lg made the graded set the product and it reads excellent (F.20).

- [x] Go's L2 grades the served calls: the typed pass's edges (goTypedEdges, in process) and the extracted nodes' Calls become `callgraph.BuildBoth` pairs, scored against the truth pairs whose callee the index defines; a dispatch edge per implementer collapses to one pair by method name, an unresolved function value and an instantiation give none and are counted, a call to a symbol the subject does not define leaves the truth and is counted, and goextract's pairs (option A) ride in the notes beside the graded set {id: LB-F176, symbol: scoreGoCalls, package: cmd/indexbench, reachable_from: TestCmdLangOracleGoTypedCalls, needs: "LB-F58,LB-F64"}
- [x] The SCIP scorer marks the truth pairs whose callee symbol the index defines in the subject (a document's definition or a local), so a harness can tell a call a service can serve from a call into the standard library or a dependency {id: LB-F177, symbol: ScoreSCIPWith, package: internal/langbench, reachable_from: TestScoreSCIPCallInIndex, needs: "LB-F64"}

### F.20 Go's served calls are the graded calls (wave lg, item go)

The owner-architect accepted design §1.6's decision (ADR-048 amendment 2026-10-02, decision (b)): Go's L2 grades the
calls the product serves. This section makes the graded set the product: E and D hold inside the served graph
(`callgraph.BuildBoth`, every `Build` plus `AddTypedEdges`, every package patch), with no store table, cache or job.
O475 records which surface reads which graph; symbol_context's lists move to the served graph. Go reads L2 excellent
(.9573 / .9219); LB-Q-go-rest carries the rest. Every row of the 90 plans reads the same before and after (2,694 rows, 355
failing; O478).

- [x] D: a syntax-only edge to a Go type or interface is a reference (`core.EdgeTypeRef`, in memory only, classified from the callee node's kind): `CallersDetail`, `CalleesDetail` and the L2 pairs leave it out, `ReferrersDetail`, `ReferencesDetail` and the dependents views return it labelled, and the reach walkers, `blast_radius` (`references`, by_depth `edge_kind`), review, the console and findings neighborhoods still read it; symbol_context answers from the served graph with `referenced_by` and `references` {id: LB-F200, symbol: Graph.edgeLabel, package: internal/analysis/callgraph, reachable_from: TestServedCalls_Golden, needs: "LB-F199"}
- [x] go.json is graded on the new served set (C, read through `CalleesDetail`) and its notes carry A, A resolved, B, the pre-lg C built explicitly, D, E and F, so every earlier option stays measurable {id: LB-F201, symbol: goCallPairSets, package: cmd/indexbench, reachable_from: TestCmdLangOracleGoTypedCalls, needs: "LB-F176,LB-F178,LB-F199,LB-F200"}

### F.23 CUE (wave fx, item cue)

CUE's first reading (LB-Q-cue, T6), against the cue binary itself: calibration/oracles/cue runs `cue lsp`
(document symbols, and its definition answer at every identifier) behind a `cue vet` gate, tier A, on cue-lang/cue's
own CI module (v0.16.1), monogon/NetMeta and this repository's schema/. The incumbent extractor read L1 unmet
(sym_recall .1278: a field inside a conjunction's, a list's or a comprehension's struct, or past six levels, was no
entity) and L2 unmet (call_recall .1751: only references to a #definition were recorded); the dialect reads L1 and L2
excellent. L3 and L4 are not attempted: a language's L3 is graded through the file resolver, which has no CUE import
strategy, and L4 through a SCIP index, which no CUE producer writes (LB-Q-cue-rest). CUE imports and cross-file
references reach ingest as doc-xref edges, which item timoni builds on.

- [x] The CUE dialect (internal/extractor/dialects/cue: native engine, `.cue`, kinds definition, field, package and import, the tool oracle's definition and field), the native registry's hook, and extractCUE as its DocResulter: a package entity per package clause (`package/<name>`, the file's root entity, apart from a field of the package's name), an import entity per string-literal import, `import 42` none {id: LB-F211, symbol: extractCUE, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCUEDialectGoldens}

### F.24 Timoni (wave fx, item timoni)

Timoni modules and bundles are plain CUE with a contract (`timoni.cue`, `values.cue`, `templates/*.cue`, `bundle.cue`,
`runtime.cue`), so the dialect is native and composes the CUE extraction: every CUE node and reference edge is the CUE
extractor's, unchanged, and the Timoni entities, calls and cross-file references ride on top. It edits neither
`cueextract.go` nor `typedcue.go` (item cue owns them); the composed extractor takes the CUE extractor's own
cross-file references when that extractor reports any. The oracle is the `timoni` CLI (offline: no cluster, no
registry pull), tier A for the cross-file references and tier C for definitions and references (a CUE syntax walk the
CLI gates, calibration/oracles/timoni); a bundle instance whose module is an `oci://` url needs a registry, so it has no truth and is counted
external (the subject's own `podinfo.cue`); the reading adds a bundle with `file://` instances to the scratch copy so
the bundle-to-module references have a subject.

- [x] A .cue file of a Timoni module (a timoni.cue, a file under a timoni.cue but never under cue.mod/, which holds the module's vendored dependencies), a bundle (a top-level `bundle:`) or a runtime (`runtime:` with a v1alpha1 apiVersion) is claimed by ingest and read through the Timoni extractor: classifyCUE tags it ToolTimoni, resolveExtractor and ReingestFile both call timoniDispatch, and every other .cue file reads as before, never gated out by the dialect's manifest (no sniff: a match.sniff on .cue would make every unmatched .cue ambiguous) {id: LB-F216, symbol: timoniDispatch, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestResolveExtractor_Timoni, needs: "LB-F23"}
- [x] The Timoni extractor composes the CUE extractor and adds the Timoni entities (timoni/module, timoni/values, timoni/object/#Def for a definition with a literal Kubernetes kind, timoni/bundle, timoni/bundle/instance/<n>, timoni/runtime, timoni/runtime/cluster/<n>), the L2 calls (a struct-literal values: to #Config.<key>, a template that takes `#config: #Config` to #Config) and the label sets that join an object template to the #Instance that renders it {id: LB-F217, symbol: TimoniExtractor.ExtractDoc, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestTimoniDialectGoldens, needs: "LB-F216"}
- [x] A bundle instance's `module.url` of `file://<path>` is a repo-path reference to the module's timoni.cue (the path read from the bundle file's directory, as Timoni reads it), and #Instance is a label-selector reference, scoped by the module root, to each object template it names, so ingest stores the instance-to-module and instance-to-template edges; an oci:// url, a non-literal url, an absolute path and one that leaves the repository are counted by reason (TimoniUnresolvedRefs), never guessed {id: LB-F218, symbol: timoniWalk.moduleRef, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestTimoniBundleModuleRefs, needs: "LB-F217"}

### F.29 Dart to L4 (wave lx, item dart)

Dart read L3 good (file_edge_recall .3534) and L4 unmet (typed_recall .8464) at wave lb. The L3 miss was mostly the
oracle's: scip-dart numbers `local N` symbols per document, and the file-edge truth read them as global (577 of 1,194
edges), so the truth edge count falls to 617. The dialect's own share is Dart's `part` / `part of`: a library's parts
are its own names. L4 is S1 (LB-F239, item python), cherry-picked. What no dialect query reaches is filed, not built:
O533 (doc-comment references), O534 (a part reads its library's imports), O535 (lower case names, `show` names), O536
(typed receivers, a reference outside any definition), O537 (an import-prefix target in typed_recall).

The records are O538..O543.

### F.31 Ruby (wave lx, item ruby)

Ruby read L1 excellent and L2 and L3 good, each capped by its oracle's tier. Wave lx took the extraction first. A bare
identifier is now a call unless a local binds it: the tags engine's locals rule, the manifest's `queries.locals`
(LB-F254, LB-F255). Tier-C L2 on sinatra v4.2.1 + jekyll v4.4.1 moved from .8688/.9938 to .9944/.9942, every remaining
miss classified. Then wave lx measured both tier-A routes:

- scip-ruby on two Sorbet-typed gems indexed with their own sorbet/config (LB-F257, O545);
- a runtime require trace that Ruby itself evaluates (LB-F256, design §1.3, O547).

The re-pin rule was committed before measuring: take the typed gems only if their reading proves L3 and L1 excellent.
They lose L3 (file_edge_recall .0613 and .0794, the resolver's constant binding, O546), so sinatra and jekyll stay
graded and the typed numbers are the ceiling (LB-F258). Against the trace, the dialect reads L3 unmet on the graded
subjects (source roots the resolver lacks, O547). The graded L3 stays the tier-B dump's, and LB-Q-ruby-rest carries
both ceilings. Epoch need for the integrator: every `.rb`, `.rake`, `.ru` and Rakefile's calls change (bare-identifier
calls added), so Ruby needs the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump. `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited
here.

- [x] A bare identifier (`helper`, `params` in `params.fetch`: no receiver, no arguments) is a Ruby call unless a local of that name was written at or before it in a scope it sees: the tags engine's locals rule reads `@local.scope` and `@local.definition` from the dialect's locals query in the same walk as the tags query, keeps a candidate of a pattern set `(#set! is-not "local")` only when no definition in its scope chain binds it (def, class, module, `class << self` and `def self.x` are gates; blocks and lambdas see out) and no other pattern names its node; every other dialect's output is byte-identical {id: LB-F254, symbol: keepNonLocalCalls, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestRubyLocalsRule}
- [x] A dialect manifest's `queries.locals` (dialects.go, schema/dialect with its valid and negative testdata) names the locals query, appended to the tags query at compile; a tags pattern set is-not "local" with no locals query, or a locals query no pattern uses, is refused at registry build {id: LB-F255, symbol: tsDialectRules.loadLocalsQuery, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestRubyLocalsManifest}
- [x] A runtime require trace (`calibration/oracles/ruby-require-trace`): each subject file loaded in its own child under the subject's own bundle and test load path, every require, require_relative, load and autoload that runs recorded with the file Ruby's lookup names, written as neutral imports, tier A when the executed literal statements are at least 90% of the in-repo ones (design §1.3); sinatra .9864, jekyll .9580, ruby-lsp .9793, spoom 1 {id: LB-F256, oracle: lexical, path: calibration/oracles/ruby-require-trace/ruby_require_trace.rb, pattern: "ratio >= 0.90", needs: "LB-F41"}

semantic model as a tier-A calls and file-edge oracle beside it (LB-F287, O591, O592), reads scip-dotnet's references
at their written sites (LB-F286), and takes the dialect's real extraction further: constructions are calls (LB-F288),
fields, events, enum members, delegates and indexers are definitions and a property is named by its type, so a
same-named type binds again (LB-F289, O593, O595). The reading meets L4 with every level excellent. A Unity project is
measured at L1 and a MonoBehaviour's Unity messages are entrypoints (LB-F290). Epoch need for the integrator: every
`.cs` file's extraction changes (O594); `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here. The records are O590..O595.

### F.33 C and C++ (wave lf1, item ccpp)

C++ is dispatched: the `cpp-macros` pre-pass read the decorator and try/catch macros the grammar misparsed (O455), lang-parity read MERGE against `cppextract.go`, and fusion serves C++ from its dialect (LB-F117). The include-path resolver then had to read the dialect's namespaced names (O596) and stop binding a member call by its receiver's name (O523, O597), and it follows an `#include` only where the build's preprocessor can keep it (O468). Both dialects name fields, enumerators and file- or namespace-scope variables (O367), and the harness reads scip-clang's L4 over what an extractor can name in the files a reading measures (O598) and leaves out, under LB-F203's cap, the file pairs the index cannot judge (O599). C reads L4 excellent at every level and C++ L1 and L2 excellent with L3 and L4 good, both level_met L4 (the code of fathom 085a1b86); C++'s CPU per byte sits at the 1 MB/s tenancy floor under load (O600), so its reading was taken on a calm machine. The records are O596..O602. Epoch need for the integrator: every `.cc .cpp .cxx .hpp .hh .hxx` file and every C++ `.h` (dispatch: namespaced names, macros, aliases, unions, members, receiver calls) and every `.c` and C `.h` (fields, enumerators, variables) is re-read, the wave's one `ExtractorEpoch` bump; `internal/fusion/extractor_epoch.go` is not edited here.

does not hold, so the extractor is left as it is.

### F.36 Prometheus and PromQL (wave c1, item prom)

Observability as code becomes a measured tier (T7, ADR-062): Prometheus rule files, configuration and promtool tests are
read by the doc engine, and their PromQL is read by one shared pure-Go selector scanner, `internal/promql`, never a
main-module dependency: the Prometheus parser checks it from the nested, test-only module `calibration/oracles/prometheus`
(1,451 corpus expressions agree, none disagree, at thanos v0.42.4, kube-prometheus v0.19.0, node_exporter v1.12.1 and
opentelemetry-demo 3.1.0). The doc engine's promql mode turns a selector's identifying-label equality matcher into a
route-host value (design §4 "PromQL references"), so a rule's `job="checkout"` meets the `deploy:` key the checkout
Service or compose service provides; a regex or negative matcher is counted, never guessed. The records are O620..O624.

- [x] `internal/promql` reads every vector selector of a PromQL expression (metric name, `=`/`!=`/`=~`/`!~` matchers, written span) through string escapes, comments, `{__name__=...}` and quoted UTF-8 names, by/without/on/ignoring/group_left/group_right label lists, offset and `@`, subqueries, functions, aggregations, binary operators and colon recording-rule names, never evaluating; `IdentityLabels()` is the universal default identifying set as embedded data (K15); the Prometheus parser's ExtractSelectors agrees on every corpus expression it parses (the differential lives in a nested test-only module, ADR-020/025) {id: LB-F316, symbol: Selectors, package: internal/promql, reachable_from: TestSelectors}
- [x] The doc engine's promql mode: a reference's `promql` records the metric names an expression reads (a `record:` name another rule uses, L2), and a route-host cross-file reference's `promql` makes each selector's equality matcher on an identifying label one route value scoped by its namespace matcher, else the reference's scope, in the DNS form the route resolver reads; regex, negative and unscannable are `DocResult.Unresolved`, by reason; `schema/dialect` validates it (negatives 26-30) {id: LB-F317, symbol: promqlRouteRefs, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestDocPromQLRouteRefs, needs: "LB-F316"}
- [x] Tier T7, observability as code: `schema/langqueue` #Tier and the queue loader's program tiers accept T7 and still refuse anything past it (T8), design §18.3 names it {id: LB-F318, symbol: IsProgramTier, package: internal/langbench, reachable_from: TestParseQueueRefusesBadEntries}


### F.36 GitHub Actions at dispatch parity, Composer, and the late-YAML hub (wave c1, item ci)

Wave c1 item ci owns the fusion dispatch hub the Prometheus and OTel Collector items merge: a late-YAML signal as
classifyYAML's last verdict (`lateYAMLDetects`), claiming no workflow and no Kubernetes object, nil-safe until a
dialect's manifest is in the build (LB-F309). Then GitHub Actions moves to the dialect at lang-parity parity with run-step,
pin and image edges (LB-F306, LB-F310, LB-F311), and `composer.json` is read as a manifest (LB-Q-composer).

- [x] A YAML file no earlier classifier signal claims is read by the first late dialect (prometheus, then otel-collector) whose manifest glob or content sniff claims it and which decodes to a root; never a workflow file or a Kubernetes object, and with no late dialect in the build nothing is claimed; ingest, ReingestFile and ContentDialect agree {id: LB-F309, symbol: lateYAMLDetects, package: internal/fusion, reachable_from: TestIngest_LateYAMLReach}

## Q. The queue — one row per `language-breadth.queue.json` entry, in queue order

Each row's contract is its reading's computed `level_met` (`L[234]` accepts the target or
above). The row title names the engine, grammar source and licence, the oracle(s), and the
incumbent it retires. Workers follow design §13; the needs list is the queue entry's
`depends_on`.

: workspace:^` specs that yarn's listing reports as mismatched (babel's own yarn plugin picks the branch only when it resolves) are the only dialect edges in no truth; a string-valued bin is not an entity (the 2 sym_recall misses: npm names its route after the package); the dialect reads `workspaces` only as an array of paths, so the object form, ! exclusions and a pnpm-workspace.yaml or lerna.json member list are not read (six of next.js's seven pnpm patterns name directories outside the corpus checkout)", trigger: "a join that takes a dependency name only to a workspace member (a connector exclusion for vendored and fixture manifests, or a package-json resolver that evaluates the root's workspaces; connector or fusion work, not the oracle's); then the reading is re-measured over every package.json"}

- [-] C++ at L4 good, below excellent (L1 and L2 excellent, L3 and L4 good): scip-clang 0.4.0 with the build's own compiler (clang-calls, tier A) over the cmake compile databases of fmtlib/fmt 12.2.0, nlohmann/json v3.12.0 (include/) and gabime/spdlog v1.17.0, universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) for L1, clang-ast-defs for range_agreement, at fathom 89b21684 (`docs/bench/readings/lang/cpp.json`, re-read 2026-10-09, wave lf1 item ccpp, the dialect dispatched since LB-F117): L1 sym_recall .9689 and sym_precision .9735, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement .9966, signature_coverage 1; L2 call_precision .9565 and call_recall .9292; L3 import_resolution 1 (257 of 257), file_edge_precision .9754 (476 of 488 judged pairs; 54 the index cannot judge left out, 9.96%, LB-F296) and file_edge_recall .4355 (476 of 1,093); L4 def_mapping .8989 (5,243 of 5,833) and typed_recall .9656 (28,112 of 29,115); throughput 1.21 MB per CPU second at a load average near 13 (0.78 to 0.96 at loads of 30 to 100, under the 1 MB/s floor, O600); lang-parity against cppextract.go reads MERGE at L4, every gated metric ahead (`docs/bench/readings/lang/parity/cpp.json`) {id: LB-Q-cpp-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L3 good, not excellent: file_edge_recall .4355 vs .50: 617 truth edges undrawn, the ones whose target the referring file's #includes never reach bounding recall near .53 (O469); json's references to abi_macros.hpp's namespace macros are lost where the cpp-macros pre-pass blanks a macro alone on its line (NLOHMANN_JSON_NAMESPACE_BEGIN; 38 truth edges); file_edge_precision .9754 holds while the oracle-blind share stays at or under LB-F203's 10% (9.96% here; above the cap it reads .8761, O599), and its 12 judged false pairs are classified in O602. L4 good, not excellent: def_mapping .8989 vs .95: 590 definitions unmapped: special members the compiler writes or a class defaults (`operator=`, `~iter_impl`, `= default` constructors, which the dialect does not tag), conversion and literal operators, using-declarations scip-clang defines as aliases (`using testing::Invoke;`), constants local to a macro-written test body (gtest TestBody, Catch2 CATCH2_INTERNAL_TEST_n), data members behind a macro the pre-pass does not read and json's basic_json behind NLOHMANN_BASIC_JSON_TPL_DECLARATION; 7,146 macro-written definitions, 13,516 out of scope (fmt's bundled gtest, json's unit tests) and the file and nameless ones are counted apart (LB-F292, LB-F293); typed_recall .9656 is excellent. L2 call_recall .9292: the calls written in a macro's body joined the truth with their callers mapped (O601). Tenancy: the cpp-macros pre-pass costs about 1.8x the dialect's CPU per byte (an error query on every file, a second parse of 138 of 232, 51 of which still do not parse), which leaves the dialect at the 1 MB/s floor on a loaded machine (O600); LB-M5 reads FAIL at 1.92x (footprint.md, 2026-10-02)", trigger: "a pre-pass shape for a template-declaration macro and Catch2's TEST_CASE with string arguments (O600), symbols for a class's defaulted special members and using-declarations, a reference kept where the pre-pass blanks a macro, and an O(1) root error check in the tags engine (O600); then cpp.json is re-read"}
- [ ] Go (.go) — language at L4 (native engine; native:go/parser+go/types, BSD-3-Clause; oracle: ctags, scip-go) {id: LB-Q-go, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/go.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L4\"", needs: "LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Go at L3 excellent, below its L4 target: measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) and scip-go 0.2.7 (tier A, `calibration/oracles/scip-go`) on spf13/cobra 40b5bc14 (the tag v1.9.1's current target) and cli/cli v2.69.0 at fathom 64ea82d9 (`docs/bench/readings/lang/go.json`, wave lf1, 2026-10-03): L1 excellent (sym_recall .9986, sym_precision .9994; LB-F60); L2 excellent, call_precision .9573 and call_recall .9219 under wave lg's served set (design §1.6, LB-F199..LB-F201), unchanged; L3 excellent, file_edge_precision .9972 and file_edge_recall .7034 with import_resolution 1 (design §1.7, LB-F297, LB-F298: the pairs only an interface-dispatch edge or an instantiation fact draws are not references the source writes, 4,097 of cli/cli's 7,136 typed-pass file pairs, 880 of them in the truth); L4 def_mapping .5494 and typed_recall .5805 {id: LB-Q-go-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L4 unmet, two metrics, one cause: scip-go defines and references what fathom's Go extractor emits no symbol for. def_mapping .5494 (5,554 of 10,521 definitions on cli/cli, 672 of 811 on cobra): 4,236 of the 4,967 misses are struct fields (and the interface methods scip-go writes the same way), 728 are package symbols (one per file's package clause), 3 other. typed_recall .5805 (54,228 of 96,460 edges): 28,084 of the 42,232 unmapped endpoints are references to a field, 14,101 to a package, 47 other. Arithmetic over the counts, not graded: def_mapping .8837 without the field class and .9995 without fields and packages, typed_recall .7931 and .9991. Go field symbols are an owner decision (neither ADR-048 nor design §1.4 makes fields-as-symbols a cross-language rule: Rust, Python, Java and C# added members per language; O606), and the per-file package symbol is the question Python's module definitions left open (O527; O607). typed_recall graded on fathom's own go/types edges was measured and is not what the design allows (the native pass is a call graph: .6062 of the import's edges between mapped definitions, a type or variable reference only as an instantiation fact; O608). L2 is excellent and its counted remainder is small: 613 served pairs the truth lacks (209 a dependency's call bound by name from a file the typed pass yielded no edge for or never compiled, 404 other, 0 a type) and 1,164 truth pairs C lacks (872 calls through a func-typed field, variable or parameter, which the typed pass records unresolved; 292 a node no edge reaches). L3 is excellent with 9 false file pairs left (4 direct, 5 function value) and recall .7034", trigger: "the owner's decision on Go field symbols (a member pass, Owner.name, a field kind outside oracle_kinds as for Python LB-F241/LB-F242; goextract changes and ExtractorEpoch bumps) and on the per-file package or module symbol (O527), then re-measure with lang-oracle (calibration/oracles/scip-go/run.sh); for L2, core.Edge gains a call site or the typed pass gives a call through a func-typed field a callee (the 872 pairs); for L3 recall, core.Edge carries the interface of a dispatch edge (the dispatch pairs then map to the interface method's file, which scip-go writes)"}

- [-] MSBuild beyond the files the doc engine decodes, and its solutions: measured against dotnet msbuild (MSBuild 18.9.11 in .NET SDK 10.0.401; -getItem, -getProperty and -preprocess; tier A, definitions, references and cross-file references) on App-vNext/Polly 8.8.0 (34 files, the whole repository) and dotnet/aspnetcore v10.0.12 (src/Http: 41 files, 40 projects and the props and targets they import), 2026-09-29: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (75 files; the 4 MSBuildTarget entities are counted under excluded_by_kind), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision .9688 (62 pairs; the 2 dialect-only references are System.Memory under a Windows-only target framework that a macOS run does not evaluate, 3 such declarations in all: the harness compares only files that hold a reference in the tool's truth, so a third file with none is left out), xref_precision 1 and xref_recall .9474 (36 of 38 edges; both misses are written from $(RepoRoot)), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent {id: LB-Q-msbuild-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the queue entry names .sln and this dialect reads none: a solution is a line-based text format and not one of the doc engine's decoders (YAML, JSON, TOML, XML), and .slnx is XML whose root is a <Solution> the engine cannot tell from a <Project> (a decoded root carries no element name), so no solution and no solution-to-project edge is indexed and `dotnet sln list` is not an oracle here; property expansion is not modelled, so a ProjectReference written with $(RepoRoot) or $(MSBuildThisFileDirectory) resolves to nothing (2 of 38 oracle edges) and no <Import> edge is drawn at all (Polly and aspnetcore write theirs with a property), a declaration under a false condition is still a reference (the oracle unions the Debug and Release evaluations of every framework the tool lists, so a framework a macOS run cannot evaluate stays out of its truth), a Directory.Build.props is imported implicitly and its items belong to the importing project for the tool and to the props file here, aspnetcore's <Reference Include=assembly> items that eng/targets/ResolveReferences.targets turns into ProjectReferences (12 oracle edges, attributed to that targets file, outside src/Http) are not modelled, and the central package versions (PackageVersion, GlobalPackageReference), Choose/When blocks, items inside a target, properties and a target's own DependsOnTargets, BeforeTargets and AfterTargets are not entities or references; aspnetcore cannot be evaluated from a clean checkout (its root Directory.Build.props and .targets import artifacts/bin/GenerateFiles/*, which its own first build step writes), so the oracle run stubs both as empty projects", trigger: "a solution decoder in the doc engine (or a root-element selector for .slnx) and a property-expanding value for ProjectReference and Import; then .sln joins the oracle through dotnet sln list, Import edges join the truth and the reading is re-measured"}

### Wave 3 — the next tier, components and the build/CI family

- [x] Kustomize (kustomization.yaml) — tool format at L3 (doc engine; pure-go:gopkg.in/yaml.v3, n/a; oracle: kustomize build (tier A); retires kustomizeextract.go) {id: LB-Q-kustomize, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/kustomize.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[34]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22,LB-F18,LB-Q-kubernetes"}
- [-] Kustomize beyond the base and overlay directories an ingest resolves today: measured against Kustomize v5.8.1 (kubectl v1.36.1's embedded build; tier A, definitions, references and cross-file references, each entry kept only when the build changes without it) on kubernetes-sigs/kustomize kustomize/v5.8.1 (examples: 18 kustomizations, 16 built by the tool) and argoproj/argocd-example-apps 8088f4c (4 files, 3 built), 2026-09-29: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (89 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (70 entries, every one evidenced by the tool), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (13 of 13 edges), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent; a scratch ingest of both subjects stores the same 13 edges {id: LB-Q-kustomize-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a directory a resource or base names resolves to the one doc file in it (design section 4), and that holds today only because an ingest dispatches a Kubernetes manifest to the incumbent K8sExtractor, which is not a doc-engine extractor: the day the kubernetes dialect is dispatched every base that holds a manifest holds two doc files and its overlay edge stops resolving (TestKustomizeBaseDirectoryNeedsTheOneFileRule pins the rule); a resource that names a manifest is recorded but resolves to nothing because no manifest has a root entity in the store yet, and the harness scores only edges between the files the dialect owns (53 oracle edges to manifests are out of scope); a remote base or patch names no path of the repository (loadHttp is left out of the measure, and no build offline can follow it); the tool cannot build 3 of the 22 kustomizations (a remote base, kustomizeconfig/mykind.yaml and all.yaml are not in the checkouts) so they are not measured; transformers, generators, validators, configurations, crds, replacements, replicas and helmCharts are not entities or references; a patch is signed by its target name and an image by its newName because the doc engine has no concatenation; the measure is small (19 files, 13 base or overlay edges) and both subjects are documentation and demonstration trees", trigger: "fusion.ResolveDocXrefs preferring the file the referring dialect owns when a directory holds several doc files (needed before the kubernetes dialect is dispatched) and a manifest root entity that a resource entry can resolve to; then the reading is re-measured on a larger subject with components, transformers and overlays of overlays"}

Measured 2026-09-29 by parsing every calibration file with the grammar and counting those whose tree holds an ERROR or MISSING node (calibration/corpus.d/assembly.yaml: cfenollosa/os-tutorial master 8002382, 71 NASM .asm files; BLAKE3-team/BLAKE3 1.8.7 c/, 8 GAS .S and 4 MASM .asm files; universal-ctags 6.2.1 reads all three syntaxes with one parser, so the L1 oracle was never the obstacle) {id: LB-Q-assembly-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real assembly under the 5% line: 69 of the 83 calibration files (83.1%: os-tutorial 57 of 71, BLAKE3 12 of 12) parse with an error under RubixDev/tree-sitter-asm v0.24.0 (5bb5b03) and under upstream main 839741f (2025-11-08, six commits later and the best pin measured, which changed neither count); it is one generic grammar and lacks the common shapes of all three dialects: GAS directives with arguments (.p2align 6, .section .note.GNU-stack,'',%progbits, .type f, @function, .equ N, 8), NASM [org 0x7c00], %include, $ and $$, quoted data (db 'hi', 0), MASM quoted segment classes ('CODE'). Re-plan when a tree-sitter grammar (or one per dialect: GAS, NASM, MASM) parses the calibration subjects with at most 5% of files in error: pin it, then build the dialect (global labels and macros, local labels .L* and numeric 1: excluded, universal-ctags Asm kinds define, label, macro, type through oracle_kinds), dispatch .s .S .asm (an ExtractorEpoch bump) and measure L1 at tier B"}

a dag task's `depends:` expression and `{{tasks.x.outputs}}` need string-interpolation capture, which the engine lacks (appfw's optional pattern capture); a templateRef to a WorkflowTemplate that writes metadata.namespace is named WorkflowTemplate/<ns>/<name> and no selector reaches it from a nested step; an Application's source.path resolves only to a directory of this repository holding exactly one Argo file and names a directory of another repository (repoURL) in every Application of the subjects, so the path and chart edges have no measured truth; argocd-example-apps ships no LICENSE file (the argoproj organisation's Apache-2.0 is the declaration, as the kustomize shard records it)", trigger: "an offline tool that evaluates Argo CD Applications, ApplicationSets and AppProjects (then argo-cd and argocd-example-apps rejoin the tier-A reading); appfw's pattern capture lands (depends and {{tasks.x}} as L2 references); a node-parent namespace in the doc engine (a nested reference reading the object's namespace); a subject whose Applications name directories of their own repository, with one manifest file each"}

- [-] Groovy refused (design §14.2): no dialect is built and no level is claimed; .groovy and .gvy stay disclosed as unsupported. Measured 2026-10-01 with the tree-sitter 0.27 CLI over every .groovy and .gvy file of the queue's subjects (calibration/corpus.d/groovy.yaml: spockframework/spock spock-2.4, 576 files; apache/grails-core v7.2.4, 5,132), a file counting when its tree holds an ERROR or MISSING node; universal-ctags 6.2.1 has no Groovy parser, so L1 would have been tier C {id: LB-Q-groovy-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Groovy under the 5% line: murtaza64/tree-sitter-groovy main 2a6ddd55 (the queue's grammar, MIT, the most recent commit 2026-09-27) leaves an error in 3,642 of 5,708 files (63.8%: spock 331 of 576, grails-core 3,311 of 5,132), dekobon/tree-sitter-groovy v0.3.0 (654e4c2d, Apache-2.0 or MIT, the best of the four on Groovy sources) in 2,466 (43.2%), Decodetalkers/tree-sitter-groovy master 679459be (MIT, generated with the CLI) in 4,326 (75.8%) and amaanq/tree-sitter-groovy v0.1.2 (70efb0b9, MIT declared) in 4,875 (85.4%); the language-pack catalogue's Decodetalkers pin a6cf8f80 carries no licence at that commit and lang-pin -tree refuses it; the grammars read Groovy as a scripting language and fail on its class syntax: an implements clause, a constructor, a throws clause, annotation arguments (named values, class literals), wildcard generics (Closure<?>). Re-plan when a tree-sitter Groovy grammar parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error: pin it, build the dialect (classes, interfaces, traits, enums, methods, constructors, fields, closures assigned to names), dispatch .groovy and .gvy (an ExtractorEpoch bump) and measure L1 and L2 at tier C"}

- [-] MATLAB below L1, and dispatched: a `.m` file whose first KiB opens with a function, a classdef or a % comment is read by the tree-sitter dialect (acristoffers/tree-sitter-matlab v1.3.1, MIT: classes, properties, methods and functions, each callable with a signature and range; 8 of 3,180 calibration files parse with an error) and an Objective-C `.m` is never claimed; no level is claimed because universal-ctags 6.2.1 cannot prove L1 for it {id: LB-Q-matlab-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "blocked on an oracle (design §14.2): ctags 6.2.1 reports MatLab classes, functions and variables only, so a method is an unscoped function and a property is a variable among 45,154 tagged assignments of chebfun v5.7.0 and export_fig v3.58 (3,179 files); required_kinds keeps class, function, method and property whole, method and property fall outside the kind intersection, and lang-oracle refuses the reading (design §1.4: required kind method is outside the kind intersection). For the record, on an uncommitted scratch manifest that compares classes and functions only (no reading, no level): sym_recall .8758, sym_precision .9907, kind_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, 2.45 MB/CPU-s, 433 peak module pages, 0 faults (dialect notes: internal/extractor/dialects/matlab/PROVENANCE.md); ctags also misses 12 classes and writes no MatLab end line, so range_agreement would cap L1 at good even with an oracle for the kinds", trigger: "an L1 oracle that reports the methods and properties of a classdef (a MATLAB parser dump or a SCIP producer): measure the four required kinds against it, then flip LB-Q-matlab; L2 needs a call oracle as well"}

the other is a call to a task the file never declares), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (14 of 14 include edges), so L1, L2 and L3 excellent; a scratch ingest of the three subjects stores 13 of the 14 edges {id: LB-Q-taskfile-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a directory an include names resolves to the one doc file in it (design section 4), and the harness resolves over the files the dialect owns while an ingest resolves over every file a doc-engine extractor reads: go-task/task's `taskfile: ./website` names a directory that holds a Taskfile beside a package.json, so the bench scores that edge and an ingest drops it (ingest-side xref_recall .9286, over the L3 excellent line of .80; TestTaskfileDirectoryNeedsTheOneFileRule pins the rule); an include of the repository root as a directory names `.`, which repo-path does not resolve, so only the file form does; an included Taskfile under another name (Taskfile.docker.yml, tasks/build.yml) is not one of go-task's eight basenames and is neither read nor an include target; a namespaced call, an alias and a label are names that match no local task; runtime-radar's kube-manager test-docker calls test-docker-cleanup, which no file declares: the dialect draws it and the tool cannot run it (the one ref_precision miss); the queue's two subjects are thin (the connector never reads testdata, where go-task/task keeps 180 of its 183 Taskfiles) so Runtime-Radar/runtime-radar is added to them and 13 of the 14 scored edges are its", trigger: "fusion.ResolveDocXrefs preferring the file the referring dialect owns when a directory holds several doc files; then the reading is re-measured with a subject that includes directories, and the ingest count read against the reading's"}

the 43 OPA rejects, v0 syntax and examples written to fail, are read and not measured), 2026-10-01: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (2,290 definitions), kind and range agreement 1, signature coverage 1, call_recall and call_precision 1 (2,481 pairs), import_resolution 1 (570 of 570), file_edge_precision .9949, file_edge_recall .3858, so L1 and L2 excellent and L3 good; no file needed a repair (0 of 370 OPA-accepted files) {id: LB-Q-rego-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "file_edge_recall .3858 (581 of 1,506 import-dump pairs) is under the excellent .50: an import of a package split across files names every file of it (regal's data.regal.ast is six files and 160 files import it, data.regal.config two), while a file-resolved edge reaches only the file defining a rule the importer calls or references, so the pairs of files imported and never used stay out; the truth is what the import statements name (LB-F57), not what references reach; 3 of opa's 4 pairs are a key collision (two format testfiles of one directory declaring one package and rule name share a SymbolKey, so a same-file call is scored to the other file); a bare name that refers to a rule of the same package and a reference through an import outside a call are not L2 calls (OPA's compiler, not its parser, tells them from locals); L4 has no producer (no Rego SCIP indexer)", trigger: "the file resolver draws an import statement's own edges, from the importing file to each file of what it names, as LB-F57's truth counts them (an Opus row in fusion's file resolver, for every language at once); or a Rego SCIP producer exists; then L3 is re-measured with the committed oracle"}

- [-] Gradle builds beyond L2, and the Kotlin DSL: the Groovy DSL dialect (internal/extractor/dialects/gradle, every *.gradle script) is measured against Gradle's own evaluation (calibration/oracles/gradle, tier A) on spring-projects/spring-boot v4.1.1 (442 scripts Gradle evaluated) and apache/kafka 4.1.0 (2), 2026-10-01: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9989 (6,089 definitions), ref_recall .9993, ref_precision .999 (2,881 pairs; the 89 task dependsOn and finalizedBy pairs alone read recall .9888, precision .9670), so L1 and L2 excellent; L3 is measured and unmet {id: LB-Q-gradle-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "xref_recall .0033 (9 of 2,703 oracle cross-file references, include and project dependency to the named project's build script) at xref_precision 1: imports.scm resolves every path to its script by Gradle's default layout (namespace-dir over ':'), but the file resolver binds a call through a whole-module import only to the target file's one top-level symbol, and a build script declares many (plugins, dependencies, tasks) and none named by its project path (TestIngest_GradleBuildReachesTheGradleDialect pins the rule); a projectDir or buildFileName override and a relative project('x') are not resolved either; the Kotlin DSL (build.gradle.kts, settings.gradle.kts: spring-boot's 53 scripts, every okhttp script) stays with the kotlin dialect, because a second dialect on the kotlin blob needs its own queue entry and the file resolver keys a file by extension, so a .kts script would resolve under kotlin's strategy (fusion's resolver and the engine, not this item's files); the grammar reads 16 of the 444 measured scripts with an error (3.6%), but 82 of grails-core v7.2.4's 343 build scripts (23.9%) and 6 of spock-2.4's 25, mostly a comma between a project dependency and its closure; LB-Q-gradle also needs LB-Q-kotlin, open at its L4 target (G2-6)", trigger: "a file-resolved binding from an import to its target file as a whole (a root entity per file the resolver can bind a call to, an Opus row in fusion's file resolver), then L3 re-measured with the committed oracle; a gradle-kts dialect on the kotlin blob (queue entry, basenames build.gradle.kts and settings.gradle.kts, a file-resolve key that is not the extension) measured with the same oracle on JetBrains/Exposed and square/okhttp; LB-Q-kotlin at L4 for this row"}
- [x] Gradle build scripts in the Groovy DSL read by a tags dialect on the pinned groovy grammar and dispatched by ingest (every *.gradle script, which fell through to no extractor before): settings includes, dependencies (string notations and project paths, in dependencies and buildscript blocks), plugins (plugins-block ids and apply plugin:) and tasks with their dependsOn and finalizedBy, each include and project dependency resolved to the named project's build script through imports.scm where the file resolver binds it (TestIngest_GradleBuildReachesTheGradleDialect); reading level_met L2 {id: LB-F94, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/gradle.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[234]\""}

- [-] CUE beyond L2: the dialect (internal/extractor/dialects/cue) is measured against the cue binary v0.16.1 (calibration/oracles/cue, tier A: `cue lsp` behind a `cue vet` gate) on cue-lang/cue v0.16.1 internal/ci (16 files), monogon/NetMeta b93a56d1 (32) and this repository's schema/ at 90f0a01f (29), 2026-10-02: sym_recall 1, sym_precision 1, kind and range agreement 1 (22,020 definitions), call_recall 1, call_precision .9979 (931 pairs), so L1 and L2 excellent; L3 and L4 are not attempted {id: LB-Q-cue-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "import_resolution, file_edge_precision and file_edge_recall not measured: a language's L3 is graded only through the file resolver (a manifest naming an import strategy, a FileSpecExtractor, the harness's fileResolveLang), whose closed strategy set has no CUE module-path strategy; def_mapping and typed_recall not measured: L4 is scored only from a SCIP index, and no CUE SCIP producer exists, though the cue binary's language server answers definition with file and line (the oracle reads it); meanwhile CUE's cross-file references reach ingest through the cue-import and cue-package doc-xref strategies (LB-F214), counted in the reading, not graded for a language: precision 1, recall .7510 (359 of 478), the rest references CUE settles only by evaluating (a field of a definition another package embeds, repo.writeWorkflows reaching base)", trigger: "a cue-module import strategy in fusion's file resolver (resolvestrategy.go, wave ld's file) with CUEExtractor as a FileSpecExtractor and a cue entry in indexbench's fileResolveLang (langoracle.go), then L3 measured against the imports calibration/oracles/cue already writes; a SCIP writer over cue lsp's definition answers, or an upstream CUE SCIP producer, then def_mapping and typed_recall for L4"}

- [-] Terragrunt at L3, definitions and references good, cross-file references excellent: measured 2026-10-02 on gruntwork-io/terragrunt-infrastructure-live-example d106b47 (16 files) and gruntwork-io/terragrunt test/fixtures f7104ac (1,185 of its 1,476 .hcl files; 126 units Terragrunt could not evaluate and 13 files the parser rejects are left out), sym_recall and sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1 (3460 entities), ref_recall 1 and ref_precision 1 (362 pairs), xref_precision 0.9876 and xref_recall 0.9917 (479 of 483 truth pairs found, 479 of 485 found in truth) against python-hcl2 8.1.4 (tier C: definitions and references) and terragrunt 1.1.6 render --json --with-metadata and find --json --include (tier A: include, dependency, terraform.source and input edges) {id: LB-Q-terragrunt-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "L1 and L2 capped at good: no tool reports a Terragrunt file's entities or the references between them with lines, so the independent parse is tier C (§1.3); wave ox measured a tier-A attestation (calibration/oracles/terragrunt/terragrunt_attest.py: render --with-metadata found_in_file for blocks, inputs and a unit's own locals, find --include for includes, ablation for an included file's locals, feature blocks, unnamed files and every reference; names and lines from python-hcl2, since fathom's dialect and Terragrunt share hashicorp/hcl/v2): it evidences all 74 definitions and 20 references of the live example but only 3,056 of 3,571 definitions and 246 of 342 references of test/fixtures (152 terragrunt.stack.hcl files render does not read, 78 other files no rendered unit names, 10 units that do not render, 174 included-file locals and 96 references no rendered output depends on), so the families stay at C, locals with them; L3 misses 4 truth pairs: a config_path read from another include (include.x.dependency.y.config_path, include.x.inputs.p) or from get_parent_terragrunt_dir(), and 6 extra edges where find_in_parent_folders finds the file at two ancestors and Terragrunt takes the nearer (one file cannot see which ancestors have it); an included file's relative paths are left unresolved (they are read from the including unit)", trigger: "a tier-A dump of a Terragrunt file's blocks with lines, or a stack render (terragrunt stack generate offline) and a fixtures scope the owner accepts for the attestation oracle; fusion.ResolveDocXrefs choosing the nearest of several repo-path candidates (tl); read_terragrunt_config edges, which terragrunt find --reading reports per unit, not per entity"}

- [-] HashiCorp HCL tool configs at L2 good, the grade below excellent and Vault and Consul unread: measured 2026-10-02 on docker/buildx 63076c4, moby/buildkit 82a1f40, docker/bake-action cac84ca and docker/cli 48988f5 (docker-bake files), chef/bento c884c07 (63 Packer files), hashicorp/packer-plugin-docker 0086069 and hashicorp/nomad-guides eba21ea (23 Nomad jobspecs) (100 files, 1143 entities), sym_recall and sym_precision 1, range_agreement 1, ref_recall 1 and ref_precision 1 (691 pairs) against python-hcl2 8.1.4 (tier C) {id: LB-Q-hcl-tools-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "grade capped at good; re-examined in wave ox with the owner-approved packer 1.16.1 and nomad 2.0.7 release binaries (local measuring tools only, sha256 in calibration/oracles/hcl-tools/README.md): packer inspect without plugins lists only variables and the locals it can evaluate (chef/bento: 139 input variables, 4 locals, an empty builds section; its required_plugins name nine plugins, never fetched without asking), so Packer's sources, builds, provisioners and post-processors have no tier-A truth and Packer, 63 of the 100 files, keeps the families at C; nomad job run -output parses 21 of the 22 nomad-guides jobspecs offline and docker buildx bake --print and --list resolve the bake files, so Nomad and bake could reach A (variables, locals and references by ablation) but would not lift the grade alone; docker buildx bake --list (tier A) agrees with the parse on the bake files' target and group names (matrix and private targets allowed for) but carries no lines or references; Vault and Consul configuration (storage, listener, datacenter) is not claimed, an unclaimed .hcl stays unextracted and counted ambiguous; L3 is not attempted (target L2): a Nomad task's config.image reaches the image-tag index (LB-F144) but is not measured", trigger: "the Packer plugins bento's required_plugins name approved into the oracle tools directory (then packer inspect lists sources and builds, and an ablation oracle over Packer, Nomad and bake can take every family to A); a Vault or Consul configuration subject in the fleet; an image-ref measurement of Nomad task images"}

wave ox (2026-10-02) lifted the definitions to tier A (crossplane resource validate 2.5.0, offline against Crossplane's own CRDs at v2.4.2, on the 189 of 244 files it accepts: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9944, 352 definitions, L1 excellent; 31 package metadata files have no CRD and 24 files the schemas refuse are disclosed and out of -only); references and cross-references stay tier C (63 references, 27 cross-file edges in scope, precision and recall 1), level_met L3 (good) {id: LB-Q-crossplane-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "no offline tool resolves a compositeTypeRef or functionRef (crossplane resource validate checks schemas only; crossplane composition render needs the functions' runtimes), so L2 and L3 cap at good under the tier-C walk; a composed resource in a function's input and a provider's kinds need schemas from a registry (2 composed resources unevidenced); a composite resource or claim of a user-defined API group stays Kubernetes, so the claim-to-XRD edge needs a shared Kubernetes-dialect join from a group and kind to the CRD or XRD declaring it, coordinated with wave pc's constraint-to-ConstraintTemplate join; the engine cannot split an apiVersion into its group, so a composite type is matched by kind alone (28 ambiguous kinds in crossplane/crossplane's e2e manifests draw no edge); a file pairing an XRD with its example composite resource stays Kubernetes whole (CRMixed counts 2); a managed resource's patches (fromFieldPath) are not read", trigger: "an offline evaluator of composition references (crossplane composition render with the functions' runtimes, asked first) and the function and provider schemas fetched from a registry (asked first); a shared kubernetes-dialect `instance_of` join (group and kind to CRD or XRD) landing with wave pc's constraint join; a group-aware value in the doc engine"}

testdata copied as td because Discover prunes it), 2026-10-02: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (821 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (10 written foreign-key pairs, tier C), xref_precision and xref_recall 1 (12 route pairs, a service host or upstream target to the compose service declaring it, all found by fathom), so L1 excellent, L2 good and L3 excellent {id: LB-Q-kong-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "tier C caps L2 at good: decK renders a route under its service and a plugin under its scope, so the written reference (a route's `service`, a plugin's `service` or `route`) is read from the text and only cross-checked by the tool, and a route written under its service is containment the doc engine, which reads downward from an entity, cannot draw as a reference (O347); only 10 such pairs are written across the 821 definitions. supabase/supabase's kong.yml is the DB-less loader's (acls, credentials and comments): decK refuses it, so its 14 route pairs are tier C and its definitions unattested, and it is not scored (lang-oracle would count the dialect's definitions against an empty oracle); 1,011 of the 1,358 files decK accepts in Kong/deck (224 of 437 in go-database-reconciler) are not named kong* (O348)", trigger: "a Kong-native reading of foreign keys that says which were written (Kong's own DB-less loader run on the file), or a parent-binding selector in the doc engine so a nested route's service is a reference (O347); an oracle that attests a DB-less file (decK or Kong's loader accepting acls and credentials), which would score Supabase's 14 pairs at tier C"}

- [-] Traefik static and file-provider configuration beyond L2: measured against traefik 3.7.13 (its file provider, `GET /api/rawdata` and `/api/entrypoints` on a scratch copy; tier A, calibration/oracles/traefik/README.md) on 12 repositories with Traefik files named where the manifest claims them (19 files), 2026-10-02: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9934, ref_precision 1 and ref_recall .9643 (81 of 84 pairs), so L1 excellent and L2 excellent; L3 is unmet: xref_precision .7241 and xref_recall 1 (21 route pairs, a loadBalancer server to the compose service declaring it, all found, 29 drawn) {id: LB-Q-traefik-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "xref_precision .7241 is under the .90 bar: fathom draws 8 pairs beyond the truth for wmde/wikibase-suite, joining its config/traefik-dynamic.yml routes to the five development/tests overlay stacks beside the root compose file, the route resolver's repository fallback (O435); the xref family is also tier C (Bin-hy/Vistack's route joins a Kubernetes Service read by yaml.v3), which would cap L3 at good. ref_recall .9643 clears the .95 bar only through the larger denominator: its 3 missed pairs are still written `service: x@file` and `- y@file`, the file provider's suffix, and the doc engine's value expression has no string operation to strip it (O349). Static-to-dynamic file edges are unscored: every static file in a subject names its dynamic file by a container path (/etc/traefik/dynamic.yml, /configuration), which resolves through the compose volume mount, not as a repository path. The first eight repositories joined 4 route pairs, under the 10-pair floor; ecamp/ecamp3, picodotdev/blog-ejemplos, rendiffdev/rendiff-dev and wmde/wikibase-suite were added. traefik/traefik's own docs and integration fixtures are named for what they test and are not claimed by a name", trigger: "the resolver walking ancestor directories before the repository (O435, wave ic); a doc-engine value operation that strips a provider suffix, or a dialect-side alias (O349); a compose-volume join from a container path to the mounted file"}
- [x] `indexbench lang-routes` restricts only the measured dialect's files to `-only`, never the compose and Kubernetes files a route target joins to: a route file named by -only still resolves against its compose files, and fathom's pairs and unresolved references count for the files in scope; picosh/pico's Caddyfiles (named by -only) join 20 pairs, all found {id: LB-F168, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/caddyfile.json, pattern: "\"route_truth_found\": 20"}
- [x] The proxy oracles' route rule: a compose project is a directory, so a route joins the declarations of its nearest project that declares the host (its own directory, then each ancestor), one pair per compose file of that directory that declares the service, compose files read by docker compose config; the haproxy reading takes 19 route pairs from five subjects at tier A and the envoy, kong, traefik, caddyfile and nginx oracles share the rule (calibration/oracles/*/README.md) {id: LB-F169, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/haproxy.json, pattern: "a route joins the declarations of its nearest compose project"}
- [x] The nginx oracle is tier B: crossplane 0.5.8 relabelled from C, reasoned in calibration/oracles/nginx/README.md (NGINX Inc's parser tool on the envoy README ladder, design 1.5 D2's independent parser dump), earned by nginx -T 1.31.6 loading each file in scope and crossplane parsing the same directives from its dump, disagreements and unloadable files counted out; L1 reads excellent {id: LB-F170, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/nginx.json, pattern: "crossplane 0.5.8 is tier B .LB-F170."}
- [x] C# names a generic method's callee without its type arguments: `Make<int>(2)` is `Make`, `Registry.Pair<System.Exception, string>(5)` is `Registry.Pair` (never the `string>` tail a dotted type argument left), member, static and conditional-access forms included; call_precision .7776 to .794 against scip-dotnet 0.2.14 on Polly + Newtonsoft.Json; product-reached since `.cs` is dispatched to the dialect (LB-F232, wave lh; O462 closed) {id: LB-F189, symbol: tsCalleeName, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectGenericCallees, needs: "LB-F4,LB-F65"}
- [x] C# `nameof(x)` is an operator, not a call: the dialect no longer lists it (368 of the former fathom-only pairs); product-reached as LB-F189 is (LB-F232) {id: LB-F190, symbol: tsCalleeName, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectNameofIsNotACall, needs: "LB-F4"}
- [x] C# calls are qualified under the manifest's `calls.qualify` as PHP's are (LB-F65): a member or static call is its receiver and the name (`reg.Add`, `Registry.Create`, `this.Plain`, `base.ToString`, `reg.Touch` for `reg?.Touch<int>()`), a delegate invocation its identifier, `new X(...)` no call (scip-dotnet writes it as a `.ctor` reference, O463); product-reached as LB-F189 is (LB-F232) {id: LB-F191, symbol: qualifiedCallee, package: internal/extractor, reachable_from: TestCSharpDialectQualifiedCalls, needs: "LB-F65"}
- [x] PHP's call spelling is pinned and its L2 gap is counted: the dialect writes `self::make`, `parent::run`, `Helper::assist`, `this.client.send`, `other.items` (nullsafe), a plain function and no call for `new X`, and each of the 1,869 pairs scip-php lacks was read at its written site (1,726 a method on a variable receiver; all oracle-blind, O467) {id: LB-F192, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/php.json, pattern: "L2 re-measure 2026-10-02 .wave ld"}
- [x] C# L2 re-measured with the fixed call query and the 7,498 fathom-only pairs classified: call_precision .794, call_recall .9448, 5,272 generic-method calls with explicit type arguments, 754 in #if regions, 517 delegate invocations, 176 delegate-typed members, 185 in invalid-global-code documents, 594 unclassified, each counted once (O466) {id: LB-F193, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/csharp.json, pattern: "L2 re-measure 2026-10-02 .wave ld"}

- [-] Vim script refused (design §14.2): the dialect is built and measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on junegunn/vim-plug 0.14.0 and tpope/vim-fugitive v3.7 (7 files) at L1 excellent (sym_recall .9947, sym_precision 1, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement 1, signature_coverage 1, 0 faults), but not dispatched: .vim and the vimrc basenames stay unextracted, .vim stays disclosed as unsupported and no level is claimed {id: LB-Q-vimscript-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Vim script under the 5% line: 3 of the 7 calibration files (42.9%: vim-plug's plug.vim, vim-fugitive's autoload/fugitive.vim and plugin/fugitive.vim) parse with an error under tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-vim v0.8.1, and upstream master (two commits past the tag) reads the same, on an embedded Python heredoc, a bare - ex command and a unary-plus string; the 3 definitions the dialect misses (s:update_python, the commands G and Git) sit inside those regions; Re-plan when a tree-sitter-vim commit or release parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error: re-measure, then dispatch .vim and the vimrc basenames (an ExtractorEpoch bump); vim-fugitive's licence (the terms of Vim itself, not OSI-approved) needs a decision first"}

- [-] Tcl refused (design §14.2): the dialect is built and measured against universal-ctags 6.2.1 (tier B) on tcltk/tcllib tcllib-2-0 and tcltk/tk core-9-0-4 (827 files) but reads no level (sym_recall .8047, sym_precision .7453, kind_agreement 1, range_agreement .9703, 1 fault), and is not dispatched: .tcl, .tk and .tm stay unextracted, .tcl stays disclosed as unsupported and no level is claimed {id: LB-Q-tcl-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no grammar parses real Tcl under the 5% line: 516 of the 827 calibration files (62.4%: tcllib 438 of 704, Tk 78 of 123) parse with an error under tree-sitter-grammars/tree-sitter-tcl 850a72ab, upstream main's head and the best pin measured (two older commits read 62.7% to 69.1%), because the grammar reads every braced word as a script and fails on an array element such as $a($i), a !flag word and a stray semicolon; L1 is also unmet on the metrics, mostly because ctags names a namespace by the whole path it is written with (::struct::list) and the harness compares the oracle's names unreduced, so 759 namespaces and classes never pair (a scratch run reducing them reads sym_recall .9292, sym_precision .8607); Re-plan when a tree-sitter-tcl commit parses both subjects with at most 5% of files in error, then re-measure (with the harness comparing the oracle's names by last segment as it does the dialect's) and dispatch .tcl, .tk and .tm (an ExtractorEpoch bump)"}

- [-] Serverless Framework beyond L2: measured 2026-10-02 against the Serverless Framework v3.40.0 (tier A, `calibration/oracles/serverless`: its own reader, `serverless print --format json` on a scratch copy without the frameworkVersion pin and the plugins list, and its variable parser) on serverless/examples master bb413e5d (113 of 133 files the tool evaluates; 20 need credentials, a file or a plugin it cannot load): sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (1,156 definitions), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (137 pairs), so L1 and L2 excellent; L3 is not measured {id: LB-Q-serverless-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the cross-file references are a handler's source file (src/h.main by the runtime's extension) and a ${file(...)} include; the doc-xref pass joins doc-engine files only, so a handler cannot be stored and an include only when its target is a doc-engine document (a CloudFormation resources file); serverless.ts and serverless.js are programs, not this dialect", trigger: "a doc-xref join to a non-doc file of the service (a handler, a CDK entry, a Pulumi main), or a subject whose includes are CloudFormation resource files"}

- [-] AWS CDK apps beyond cdk.json: measured 2026-10-02 on aws-samples/aws-cdk-examples main 6408f426 (247 cdk.json): definitions against the AWS CDK CLI 2.1144.0 (tier A, `calibration/oracles/aws-cdk`: `cdk context --json -vv` loads each file as every command does and logs what it read) sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (8,469 definitions); the program the app command runs against a parse of the command checked in the subject's tree (tier C) ref_recall 1, ref_precision .9907 (213 of 215), so L1 excellent and L2 good {id: LB-Q-aws-cdk-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "a CDK app's stacks and constructs live in its program, which rides its own language's dialect; the entry file is not stored as an edge (the doc-xref pass joins doc-engine files only), an app command that names no file (mvn -e -q compile exec:java) names nothing, and only running the app (cdk synth, which needs the program's dependencies) would name the program as the tool sees it; re-examined in wave ox (a 30-minute time box): no offline tool run attests the app-to-entry-program edge, since cdk synth, cdk ls and every command that runs the app need the program's toolchain and its installed dependencies (npm, pip, Maven, Go modules, dotnet per example), and an ablation of the entry file is observable only by running the app, so the 215 pairs stay a tier-C parse of the command (213 of 215 agree) and L2 stays good", trigger: "a doc-xref join to a non-doc file (the entry program), or an offline synth harness for the examples' languages (each example's dependencies installed under scratch, cdk synth run without credentials)"}

- [-] Conftest taken up 2026-10-02 (wave pc) once LB-Q-rego proved [x]: its policies, `.rego` under `policy/` or any directory a configuration names, are read by the Rego dialect (LB-F131) at Rego's level; no conftest dialect is built and no reading taken {id: LB-Q-conftest-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the L2 target has no truth to measure: a conftest.toml holds no reference within the file (its keys, policy, namespace, data, combine, parser, output, name directories and Rego namespaces in other files), so ref_recall and ref_precision have no pair on any subject, and the installed conftest (a dev build on OPA 1.15.2) has no command that reports its configuration (parse prints an input file as data, verify runs the policies' unit tests, doc renders their METADATA, test evaluates inputs); the keys are already symbols through the generic TOML extractor, so a doc dialect for them adds an L1 of two keys on the pinned subject (open-policy-agent/conftest holds one conftest.toml: policy and namespace); the queue's tags+doc engine assumed a Rego grammar, moot now that Rego is native", trigger: "the owner retargets the item: L1 for the configuration's keys on subjects with several conftest.toml files, or L3 for its references (a policy directory and a namespace to the Rego files declaring the package, which needs a doc-xref resolution that reads Rego packages: .rego is not a doc candidate and no doc strategy names a package)"}

- [-] Cedar refused (design §14.2), 2026-10-02 (wave pc): the subjects (calibration/corpus.d/cedar.yaml: cedar-policy/cedar-examples c251f7d1, 34 files, and cedar-policy/cedar-integration-tests v4.13.0, 25 files; Apache-2.0) and the tier-A oracle (calibration/oracles/cedar, cedar-policy-cli 4.13.0, which accepts all 59 files: 168 policies and templates, 84 entity types, 148 actions, 9 common types, 3 namespaces; 421 schema reference pairs; 664 scope references) are committed; no dialect is built or dispatched and .cedar and .cedarschema stay disclosed as unsupported {id: LB-Q-cedar-rest, disposition: refused, reason: "no parser in scope reads Cedar under the 5% line: chrnorm/tree-sitter-cedar 5092204d (MIT by its LICENCE, indexbench lang-pin -tree; package.json declares ISC) leaves an ERROR node in 5 of the 42 policy files the CLI accepts (11.9%: a namespaced action entity, `PhotoApp::Action::\"viewPhoto\"`, in three; an index by a string key, `x[\"Role-A\"]`; an annotation whose string spans lines) and has no grammar for the 17 schema files, so the schema half (namespaces, entity types, actions, common types: L1's required kinds) has no definitions at all; cedar-go v1.8.0 (cda92d0d, Apache-2.0, +867,680 bytes on cmd/fathom) rejects 6 of the 42 (14.3%: every file holding a template, which NewPolicySetFromBytes does not read) and its schema AST (x/exp/schema/ast) carries no source position, so no schema definition has a line to be matched or ranged by; measured with the tree-sitter CLI 0.27.0 on the pinned grammar (not the lock recipe's wasm build) and a scratch Go program on cedar-go; a reader of Cedar's own (Rego's route, ADR-048's wave-pc amendment) was outside this item's scope; L3 has no import statement to measure (import_resolution and file_edge need one), so Cedar's cross-file reach is the governed scope, a policy_ref from a policy to the schema file declaring the type or action, which the oracle writes as cross_references", trigger: "the owner admits a native Cedar reader under ADR-048's wave-pc amendment (the second language to take it, its revisit trigger), or a tree-sitter-cedar commit with a schema grammar and at most 5% of CLI-accepted files in error, or a cedar-go release that reads templates and gives schema declarations positions; then the dialect is built (policies by @id, else file:index; namespaces, entity types, actions, common types; the governed scope as policy_ref, strategy service-name), measured against the committed oracle, and dispatched with an ExtractorEpoch bump; the queue kind (language, L3 by import edges Cedar does not have) is the owner's call"}

- [-] Azure Policy beyond L2 good: measured against an independent JSON walk of the definition schema's structure (tier C, its own line-tracking JSON reader) on Azure/azure-policy 7b0fa25a (5,658 policy documents: 5,387 definitions, 271 initiatives), 2026-10-02: sym_recall 1, sym_precision .9999 (55,711 of 55,714 definitions: 3 dialect-only parameters, O394), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (21,137 `[parameters('x')]` pairs), range_agreement 1, so L1 good and L2 good; L3 (the resource-type policy_ref emission) is not measured {id: LB-Q-azure-policy-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "tier C caps every metric at good (§1.3): no Azure tool evaluates a definition offline (O391), and the JSON schema checks shape, not the entities; the resource-type policy_ref (field: type with equals, in or like) is emitted and pinned by goldens, but its targets are ARM and Bicep resources that wave iac declares, so until then the resolver reports them undeclared, and no scorer reads them here; re-examined in wave ox: Microsoft's Enterprise Policy as Code (EnterprisePolicyAsCode 11.5.8, Azure/enterprise-azure-policy-as-code, MIT) reads a definition offline only as ConvertFrom-Json and property reads (Build-PolicyPlan, Convert-PolicyToDetails: the name, the parameters, the effect), the same reading as the walk, with no source lines and no list of parameters('x') uses, and its plan builder (Build-DeploymentPlans) reads the deployed policy resources through an Azure connection; the Bicep CLI's policyDefinitions type check accepts or refuses a document and names no entity; so the walk stays tier C (never relabelled) and the ceiling stands", trigger: "an offline Azure Policy evaluator that lists a definition's entities with source lines (tier A), or the ARM/Bicep dialects merged so a resource type resolves to a declared resource"}
- [x] Cloud Custodian policies (policies YAML) — tool format at L2 (doc engine; yaml.v3, n/a; oracle: custodian validate (tier A, offline)) {id: LB-Q-cloud-custodian, oracle: lexical, path: docs/bench/readings/lang/cloud-custodian.json, pattern: "\"level_met\": \"L[234]\"", needs: "LB-F8,LB-F9,LB-F16,LB-F17,LB-F21,LB-F22"}
- [-] Cloud Custodian beyond L2 (L1 and L2 excellent): measured against c7n 0.9.53 (tier A: its own loader, schema validator and filter and action registries; lines from a PyYAML composition, tier C) on cloud-custodian/cloud-custodian bf126f38 (13 of its 19 policy files: -only drops the 4 fixtures written to fail and 2 files discovery does not read), 2026-10-02: sym_recall and sym_precision 1 (27 policies), ref_recall and ref_precision 1 (28 filter and action type pairs), range_agreement 1, so L1 excellent and L2 excellent; L3 (the resource-type policy_ref emission) is not measured {id: LB-Q-cloud-custodian-rest, disposition: deferred, reason: "the queue's filters and actions as entities are carried as the policy's references instead: an entity name is a value on its own node and a symbol key is service::package.Name, so a file's second value filter would collide (O392); the subject's policy YAML is 6 KB, so the throughput gate reads 0.68 to 2.01 MB/s run to run (O393) and the committed reading is a run that met it; policy_ref targets come from the committed tier-A registry map (LB-F140), and the resolver is the policy-reference item's", trigger: "an ancestor value in an entity name (the engine), or a larger YAML subject of Custodian policies"}
